as in Assumption
S1
.
Assumption
S2
(
iii
)–(
iv
) state the rank conditions under cluster sampling for the covariate-adjusted canonical
2sls
and
2sfe
, respectively.
The condition
\inf_{N}\lambda_{\min}(\Omega_{N,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}})>0
in Assumption
S2
(
iv
) implies that for all
N
,
\dot{Z}_{i}\neq 0
for some units, so that
\E(S_{Z,\textup{in}})>0
and
\kappa_{N}=\E(S_{Z,\textup{in}})/\sigma^{2}_{Z}>0
.

Theorems
S1
–
S2
below give the complete version of Proposition
2
.
Let
Z_{i\mid X}
denote the residual from the least-square regression of
Z_{i}
on
(1,X_{i})
.
Let
Z_{i\mid(C,X)}
denote the residual from the least-square regression of
Z_{i}
on
(C_{i},X_{i})
.
Let

\displaystyle S_{ZD\mid X}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid X}D_{i},

\displaystyle S_{ZY\mid X}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid X}Y_{i},

\displaystyle S_{ZD\mid(C,X)}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid(C,X)}D_{i},

\displaystyle S_{ZY\mid(C,X)}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid(C,X)}Y_{i}.

Theorem S1
.

Consider the covariate-adjusted canonical
2sls
,
\texttt{2sls}(Y_{i}\sim 1+D_{i}+X_{i}\mid 1+Z_{i}+X_{i})
.

(i)

\hat{\tau}_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}=\dfrac{S_{ZY\mid X}}{S_{ZD\mid X}},\quad\hat{\textup{se}}^{2}_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}=\dfrac{1}{N^{2}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{S_{ZD\mid X}^{2}}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}\left(\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}Z_{i\mid X}\cdot r_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\right)^{2}.

(ii)

As
N\to\infty
, if Assumption
S2
(
i
)–(
iii
) hold, then

\displaystyle V_{{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}},N}^{-1/2}\cdot\sqrt{N}(\hat{\tau}_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}-\tau_{\textup{c}})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1),

\displaystyle N\hat{\textup{se}}_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}^{2}/V_{{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}},N}=1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle(\hat{\tau}_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}-\tau_{\textup{c}})/\hat{\textup{se}}_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1),

where

V_{{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}},N}=\dfrac{1}{(\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}})^{2}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\var\left\{\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-e)R_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\right\}

with
R_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}=\res(A_{i}\mid 1,X_{i})
as defined in (
S18
).

Theorem S2
.

Consider the covariate-adjusted
2sfe
,
\texttt{2sls}(Y_{i}\sim D_{i}+C_{i}+X_{i}\mid Z_{i}+C_{i}+X_{i})
.

(i)

\hat{\tau}_{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}=\dfrac{S_{ZY\mid(C,X)}}{S_{ZD\mid(C,X)}}
,

\hat{\textup{se}}_{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}^{2}=\dfrac{1}{N^{2}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{S_{ZD\mid(C,X)}^{2}}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}\left(\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}Z_{i\mid(C,X)}\cdot r_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\right)^{2}
.

(ii)

As
N\to\infty
, if Assumption
S2
(
i
)–(
ii
) and (
iv
) hold, then

\displaystyle V_{{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}},N}^{-1/2}\cdot\sqrt{N}(\hat{\tau}_{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}-\tau_{\textup{c}})\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1),

\displaystyle N\hat{\textup{se}}_{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}^{2}/V_{{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}},N}=1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle(\hat{\tau}_{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}-\tau_{\textup{c}})/\hat{\textup{se}}_{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1),

where

V_{{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}},N}=\dfrac{1}{(\kappa_{N}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}})^{2}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\var\left(\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{Z}_{i}R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\right)

with
R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}=\dot{A}_{i}-\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A}
as defined in (
S20
).

The unadjusted canonical
2sls
and
2sfe
are special cases of Theorems
S1
–
S2
, where
X_{i}=\emptyset
.
We have
Z_{i\mid X}=\bar{Z}
and
Z_{i\mid(C,X)}=\bar{Z}_{g}
, so that

\begin{array}[]{lll}\hat{\tau}_{\textup{2sls}}=\dfrac{S_{ZY}}{S_{ZD}},&&\hat{\textup{se}}^{2}_{\textup{2sls}}=\dfrac{1}{N^{2}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{S_{ZD}^{2}}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}\left\{\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z})r_{i,\textup{2sls}}\right\}^{2},\\
\hat{\tau}_{\textup{2sfe}}=\dfrac{S_{ZY,\textup{in}}}{S_{ZD,\textup{in}}},&&\hat{\textup{se}}^{2}_{\textup{2sfe}}=\dfrac{1}{N^{2}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{S_{ZD,\textup{in}}^{2}}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}\left\{\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z}_{g})r_{i,\textup{2sfe}}\right\}^{2},\end{array}

(S31)

where
S_{ZY}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z})(Y_{i}-\bar{Y})
and
S_{ZD}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z})(D_{i}-\bar{D})
.

S3
Lemmas

S3.1
Basic lemmas and useful facts

Lemma
S3
below reviews some useful properties of block matrix.

Lemma S3
.

(i)

Let
P=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}
denote a block partition of the matrix
P
. If
A
is invertible, then

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}A^{-1}+A^{-1}BS_{A}^{-1}CA^{-1}&-A^{-1}BS_{A}^{-1}\\
-S_{A}^{-1}CA^{-1}&S_{A}^{-1}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\det\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\det(A)\cdot\det(S_{A}),

where
S_{A}=D-CA^{-1}B
denotes the Schur complement of
A
.

(ii)

For square matrices
A
and
D
, the eigenvalues of
\begin{pmatrix}A&0\\
0&D\end{pmatrix}
are those of
A
and
D
combined.

Lemma
S4
below reviews Holder’s inequality for the probability space.

Lemma S4
.

(i)

Holder’s inequality: For random variables
X,Y\in\mathbb{R}
and
p,q\in(1,\infty)
with
1/p+1/q=1
, we have

\E(|XY|)\leq\{\E(|X|^{p})\}^{1/p}\cdot\{\E(|Y|^{q})\}^{1/q}.

(ii)

Lyapunov’s inequality: For a random variable
X\in\mathbb{R}
and constants
0<r<s<\infty
, we have
\E(|X|^{r})\leq\{\E(|X|^{s})\}^{r/s}
.

Lemma S5
.

(i)

For
b_{1},\ldots,b_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and
q\geq 1
,

\left\|\dfrac{b_{1}+\cdots+b_{m}}{m}\right\|^{q}\leq\left(\dfrac{\|b_{1}\|+\cdots+\|b_{m}\|}{m}\right)^{q}\leq\dfrac{\|b_{1}\|^{q}+\cdots+\|b_{m}\|^{q}}{m}.

(ii)

Let
B_{1},\dots,B_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be identically distributed
p\times 1
random vectors, and let
\bar{B}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}B_{i}
. For
q\geq 1
,

\begin{array}[]{rcccl}&&\|B_{i}-\bar{B}\|^{q}&\leq&2^{q-1}\left(\|B_{i}\|^{q}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|B_{i}\|^{q}\right),\\
\displaystyle\E\left(\|B_{i}-\bar{B}\|^{q}\cdot 1_{\{\|B_{i}-\bar{B}\|>M\}}\right)&\leq&\E\left(\|B_{i}-\bar{B}\|^{q}\right)&\leq&2^{q}\cdot\E\left(\|B_{i}\|^{q}\right).\end{array}

(iii)

For a random vector
B
and constants
0<p<q
, we have
\left\{\E(\|B\|^{p})\right\}^{1/p}\leq\left\{\E(\|B\|^{q})\right\}^{1/q}
.

Proof of Lemma
S5
.

Lemma
S5
(
iii
) states the monotonicity of
L^{p}
norms, also known as Lyapunov’s inequality, as a direct implication of Lemma
S4
(
ii
).
We verify below Lemma
S5
(
i
)–(
ii
).

Proof of Lemma
S5
(
i
).

The first inequality follows from the triangle inequality of the Euclidean norm:

\|b_{1}+\cdots+b_{m}\|\leq\|b_{1}\|+\cdots+\|b_{m}\|
.

For the second inequality, let
f(x)=\|x\|^{q}
, which is convex for
q\geq 1
by compounding the convex Euclidean norm function
f_{1}(x)=\|x\|
and the convex, nondecreasing function
f_{2}(x)=x^{q}
.
Consider a random variable
B
that takes values in
\{\|b_{1}\|,\ldots,\|b_{m}\|\}
, where
\mathbb{P}(B=\|b_{m}\|)=m^{-1}
.
Then

\displaystyle\E(B)=\frac{\|b_{1}\|+\cdots+\|b_{m}\|}{m},\quad\E\{f(B)\}=\E\left(\|B\|^{q}\right)=\frac{\|b_{1}\|^{q}+\cdots+\|b_{m}\|^{q}}{m},

and it follows from Jensen’s inequality that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\|b_{1}\|+\cdots+\|b_{m}\|}{m}\right)^{q}=f\left\{\E(B)\right\}\leq\E\left\{f(B)\right\}=\frac{\|b_{1}\|^{q}+\cdots+\|b_{m}\|^{q}}{m}.

Proof of Lemma
S5
(
ii
).

Applying Lemma
S5
(
i
) with
m=2
and
(b_{1},b_{2})=(B_{i},-\bar{B})
ensures

\displaystyle\|B_{i}-\bar{B}\|^{q}\leq 2^{q-1}\left(\|B_{i}\|^{q}+\|\bar{B}\|^{q}\right).

(S32)

Applying Lemma
S5
(
i
) with
m=n
and
b_{i}=B_{i}
ensures

\displaystyle\|\bar{B}\|^{q}=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}B_{j}\right\|^{q}=\dfrac{1}{n^{q}}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}B_{j}\right\|^{q}\leq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|B_{i}\|^{q}.

(S33)

Plugging (
S33
) into (
S32
) ensures

\displaystyle\|B_{i}-\bar{B}\|^{q}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_1}+\eqref{eq:bound_2}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2^{q-1}\left(\|B_{i}\|^{q}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|B_{i}\|^{q}\right),

\displaystyle\E\left(\|B_{i}-\bar{B}\|^{q}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{q-1}\left\{\E\left(\|B_{i}\|^{q}\right)+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E\left(\|B_{i}\|^{q}\right)\right\}=2^{q}\cdot\E\left(\|B_{i}\|^{q}\right).

∎

Lemma
S6
below reviews several standard results in probability theory.
Lemma
S6
(
i
) follows from Markov’s inequality and the first Borel–Cantelli lemma.
Lemma
S6
(
iia
) states the equivalence between convergence in distribution and convergence in probability when the limit is a constant; see, e.g.,
Van der
Vaart 2000
. Lemma
S6
(
iib
) follows from Markov’s inequality.

Lemma S6
.

(i)

For a nonnegative random variable
B\geq 0
,

\E(B)=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$B=0$ almost surely}.

(ii)

Let
(B_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence of random variables. As
n\to\infty
,

(a)

if
B_{n}\rightsquigarrow b

for a constant
b
, then

B_{n}=b+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

(b)

if
\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\E(|B_{n}|)<\infty
, then
B_{n}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

A collection of random variables
\{B_{n}\in\mathbb{R}:n\in\mathcal{C}\}
is
uniformly integrable
if

\displaystyle\lim_{M\to\infty}\sup_{n\in\mathcal{C}}\E\left(|B_{n}|\cdot 1_{\{|B_{n}|>M\}}\right)=0.

Lemma
S7
below reviews properties of uniform integrability for a set of random vectors. In particular, Lemma
S7
(
i
) implies the definition of uniform integrability for a collection of random variables in terms of
L^{p}
norms.

Lemma S7

(Uniform integrability)
.

Let
\{B_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:n\in\mathcal{C}\}
be a collection of
p\times 1
random vectors for
p<\infty
.

(i)

Let
\|\cdot\|_{a}
and
\|\cdot\|_{b}
be two norms on
\mathbb{R}^{p}
. If
\{\|B_{n}\|_{a}:n\in\mathcal{C}\}
is uniformly integrable,
then so is
\{\|B_{n}\|_{b}:n\in\mathcal{C}\}
.

(ii)

\{B_{n}:n\in\mathcal{C}\}
is uniformly integrable if and only if each coordinate set

\{B_{n,k}:n\in\mathcal{C}\}
,
k=1,\dots,p
, is uniformly integrable.

(iii)

Let
c\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be a fixed
p\times 1
vector.
If
\{B_{n}:n\in\mathcal{C}\}
is uniformly integrable, then
\{c^{\top}B_{n}:n\in\mathcal{C}\}
is uniformly integrable.

(iv)

\{B_{n}:n\in\mathcal{C}\}
is uniformly integrable if
\sup_{n\in\mathcal{C}}\E(\|B_{n}\|^{r})<\infty
for a constant
r>1
.

Lemma
S8
reviews properties of the minimum eigenvalue of symmetric positive semidefinite matrices.

Lemma S8
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
be a symmetric positive semidefinite matrix.
Let
C\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
be a matrix, and let
\|C\|_{2}
denote the largest singular value of
C
, also known as the spectral norm, with
\|C\|^{2}_{2}=\lambda_{\max}(C^{\top}C)=\lambda_{\max}(CC^{\top})
.
Then

(i)

\|Cx\|\leq\|C\|_{2}\,\|x\|
for any
q\times 1
vector
x
;

(ii)

\lambda_{\min}(C^{\top}AC)\in\big[\lambda_{\min}(A),\lambda_{\max}(A)\big]\cdot\lambda_{\min}(C^{\top}C)
.

(iii)

If
A
is a fixed matrix, and
C
is a random matrix such that
\mathbb{E}(C^{\top}C)
exists, then

\lambda_{\min}\left\{\E(C^{\top}AC)\right\}\in\big[\lambda_{\min}(A),\lambda_{\max}(A)\big]\cdot\lambda_{\min}\left\{\mathbb{E}(C^{\top}C)\right\}.

S3.2
Frisch–Waugh–Lovell type results for 2SLS

Lemma
S9
below reviews
Basu 2024
on the Frisch–Waugh–Lovell (FWL) type results for
2sls
.

Lemma S9
.

Let
D_{i}^{*},Z_{i}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
and
W_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be vectors defined for
i\in\mathcal{I}
.
Consider the
2sls
regression of
Y_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
on
(D_{i}^{*},W_{i})
instrumented by
(Z_{i}^{*},W_{i})
,

\displaystyle\texttt{2sls}(Y_{i}\sim D_{i}^{*}+W_{i}\mid Z_{i}^{*}+W_{i}).

(S34)

Let
\hat{\tau}
denote the coefficient vector of
D_{i}^{*}
, and let
r_{i}\ (i\in\mathcal{I})
denote the IV residuals.

Let
(Y_{i{\mid W}},D_{i\mid W}^{*},Z_{i\mid W}^{*})
denote the residuals from
\texttt{ols}(Y_{i}\sim W_{i})
,
\texttt{ols}(D_{i}^{*}\sim W_{i})
, and
\texttt{ols}(Z_{i}^{*}\sim W_{i})
, respectively.
Consider the
2sls
regression of
Y_{i{\mid W}}
on
D_{i\mid W}^{*}
instrumented by
Z_{i\mid W}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\texttt{2sls}(Y_{i{\mid W}}\sim D_{i\mid W}^{*}\mid Z_{i\mid W}^{*}).

(S35)

Let
\hat{\tau}_{\mid W}
denote the coefficient vector of
D_{i\mid W}^{*}
, and let
r_{i{\mid W}}\ (i\in\mathcal{I})
denote the IV residuals.
Then

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}=\hat{\tau}_{\mid W},\quad r_{i}=r_{i{\mid W}}\quad(i\in\mathcal{I}).

Lemma
S10
below builds on Lemma
S9
, and gives the explicit forms of the
2sls
coefficients and cluster-robust standard errors when
D_{i}^{*}
and
Z_{i}^{*}
are scalars.

Lemma S10
.

Assume the setting of Lemma
S9
with
q=1
.
Let
\hat{\textup{se}}
denote the cluster-robust standard error of
\hat{\tau}
from (
S34
) by Definition
1
. Let
\hat{\textup{se}}_{\mid W}
denote the cluster-robust standard error of
\hat{\tau}_{\mid W}
from (
S35
).
Let
S_{ZD\mid W}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}D_{i\mid W}^{*}
and
S_{ZY\mid W}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}Y_{i{\mid W}}
. Then

(i)

\hat{\tau}=\hat{\tau}_{\mid W}=\dfrac{S_{ZY\mid W}}{S_{ZD\mid W}}
,

\displaystyle\hat{\textup{se}}^{2}=\hat{\textup{se}}_{\mid W}^{2}=\dfrac{1}{N^{2}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{S_{ZD\mid W}^{2}}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\left(\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}\cdot r_{i{\mid W}}\right)^{2}.

(ii)

S_{ZY\mid W}
and
S_{ZD\mid W}
have the following equivalent forms:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}S_{ZY\mid W}&=&N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}Y_{i}&=&N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i}^{*}Y_{i}-\hat{\gamma}_{W,Z}^{\top}N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}W_{i}Y_{i},\\
S_{ZD\mid W}&=&N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}D_{i}^{*}&=&N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i}^{*}D_{i}^{*}-\hat{\gamma}_{W,Z}^{\top}N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}W_{i}D_{i}^{*},\end{array}

where

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}_{W,Z}=\texttt{coef}(Z_{i}^{*}\sim W_{i})=\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}W_{i}W_{i}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}W_{i}Z_{i}^{*}\right)

denotes the coefficient vector of
W_{i}
from
\texttt{ols}(Z_{i}^{*}\sim W_{i})
, with
Z_{i\mid W}^{*}=Z_{i}^{*}-W_{i}^{\top}\hat{\gamma}_{W,Z}
.

Proof of Lemma
S10
.

We verify below Lemma
S10
(
i
)–(
ii
), respectively

Proof of Lemma
S10
(
i
)

Lemma
S9
ensures that
\hat{\tau}=\hat{\tau}_{\mid W}
and
r_{i}=r_{i{\mid W}}\ (i\in\mathcal{I})
.
Given
r_{i}=r_{i{\mid W}}
, the equivalence between
\hat{\textup{se}}
and
\hat{\textup{se}}_{\mid W}
follows from the proof of
Ding 2021
.
We verify below the explicit forms of
(\hat{\tau}_{\mid W},\hat{\textup{se}}_{\mid W})
.

Consider the first stage of (
S35
),

\texttt{ols}(D_{i\mid W}^{*}\sim Z_{i\mid W}^{*})
. Let
\hat{\beta}=\texttt{coef}(D_{i\mid W}^{*}\sim Z_{i\mid W}^{*})
denote the coefficient of
Z_{i\mid W}^{*}
.
Properties of least squares ensure

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}=\dfrac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}D_{i\mid W}^{*}}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i\mid W}^{*})^{2}}=\dfrac{S_{ZD\mid W}}{S_{Z{\mid W}}},

(S37)

where

\displaystyle S_{Z{\mid W}}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i\mid W}^{*})^{2}.

(S38)

The fitted values are then

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{*}_{i{\mid W}}=Z_{i\mid W}^{*}\hat{\beta}\overset{\eqref{eq:hbzfwl}}{=}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}\cdot\dfrac{S_{ZD\mid W}}{S_{Z{\mid W}}}.

(S39)

Expression of
\hat{\tau}_{\mid W}
.

The second stage of (
S35
) equals
\texttt{ols}(Y_{i{\mid W}}\sim\widehat{D}^{*}_{i{\mid W}})
, with
\hat{\tau}_{\mid W}
equal to the coefficient of
\widehat{D}^{*}_{i{\mid W}}
. This implies

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{\mid W}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\texttt{coef}(Y_{i{\mid W}}\sim\widehat{D}^{*}_{i{\mid W}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\widehat{D}^{*}_{i{\mid W}}Y_{i{\mid W}}}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(\widehat{D}^{*}_{i{\mid W}})^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:hdisw}}{=}

\displaystyle\dfrac{\hat{\beta}\cdot\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}Y_{i{\mid W}}}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\cdot\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i\mid W}^{*})^{2}}\overset{\eqref{eq:szw}}{=}\dfrac{S_{ZY\mid W}}{\hat{\beta}\cdot S_{Z{\mid W}}}\overset{\eqref{eq:hbzfwl}}{=}\dfrac{S_{ZY\mid W}}{S_{ZD\mid W}}.

Expression of
\hat{\textup{se}}_{\mid W}
.

Let

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllllllll}Z^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}=(Z^{*}_{g1{\mid W}},\ldots,Z^{*}_{g,n_{g}{\mid W}})^{\top},&&Z^{*}_{\mid W}=(Z^{*}_{11{\mid W}},\ \ldots,\ Z^{*}_{G,n_{G}{\mid W}})^{\top}=(Z^{*\top}_{[1]{\mid W}},\ \ldots,\ Z^{*\top}_{[G]{\mid W}})^{\top},\\
&&D^{*}_{\mid W}=(D^{*}_{11{\mid W}},\ \ldots,\ D^{*}_{G,n_{G}{\mid W}})^{\top},\\
\widehat{D}^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}=(\hat{D}^{*}_{g1{\mid W}},\ldots,\hat{D}^{*}_{g,n_{g}{\mid W}})^{\top},&&\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W}=(\widehat{D}^{*}_{11{\mid W}},\ \ldots,\ \widehat{D}^{*}_{G,n_{G}{\mid W}})^{\top}=(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{[1]{\mid W}},\ \ldots,\ \widehat{D}^{*\top}_{[G]{\mid W}})^{\top}\end{array}

denote the vectorization of
Z_{i\mid W}^{*}
,
D_{i\mid W}^{*}
, and
\widehat{D}^{*}_{i{\mid W}}
over cluster
g
and all units, respectively, with

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\overset{\eqref{eq:hdisw}}{=}Z^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\hat{\beta},\quad\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W}\overset{\eqref{eq:hdisw}}{=}Z^{*}_{\mid W}\hat{\beta},\quad S_{Z{\mid W}}\overset{\eqref{eq:szw}}{=}N^{-1}Z^{*\top}_{{\mid W}}Z^{*}_{\mid W}

(S41)

from (
S38
)–(
S39
).
This ensures

\displaystyle N^{-1}\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:fwl_zd}}{=}

\displaystyle N^{-1}\hat{\beta}^{2}\cdot Z^{*\top}_{{\mid W}}Z^{*}_{\mid W}\overset{\eqref{eq:fwl_zd}}{=}\hat{\beta}^{2}\cdot S_{Z{\mid W}},

\displaystyle N(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\hat{\beta}^{2}\cdot S_{Z{\mid W}})^{-1},

so that

\displaystyle N(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}\cdot\widehat{D}^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:fwl_zd}}{=}

\displaystyle(\hat{\beta}^{2}\cdot S_{Z{\mid W}})^{-1}\cdot Z^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\hat{\beta}

(S42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\hat{\beta}\cdot S_{Z{\mid W}})^{-1}\cdot Z^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:hbzfwl}}{=}

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{S_{ZD\mid W}}\cdot Z^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}.

Let
r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}=(r_{i{\mid W}}:{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}})
denote the residual vector for cluster
g
.
Let

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}=\diag(\widehat{\Omega}_{g})_{g=1}^{G},\quad\text{where}\ \ \widehat{\Omega}_{g}=r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\cdot r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}^{\top}.

We have

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{\Omega}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{[1]{\mid W}},\ldots,\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{[G]{\mid W}})\begin{pmatrix}r_{[1]{\mid W}}\cdot r_{[1]{\mid W}}^{\top}\\
&\ddots\\
&&r_{[G]{\mid W}}\cdot r_{[G]{\mid W}}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{D}^{*}_{[1]{\mid W}}\\
\vdots\\
\widehat{D}^{*}_{[G]{\mid W}}\end{pmatrix}

(S43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\left(r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\cdot r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}^{\top}\right)\widehat{D}^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}.

In addition, let
P_{Z}=Z^{*}_{\mid W}(Z^{*\top}_{\mid W}Z^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}Z^{*\top}_{\mid W}
denote the projection matrix of
Z^{*}_{\mid W}
, with

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W}=P_{Z}D^{*}_{\mid W},\quad(D^{*\top}_{\mid W}P_{Z}D^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}=(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}.

(S44)

By Definition
1
,

\displaystyle\hat{\textup{se}}_{\mid W}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Definition~\ref{def:crse}}}{=}

\displaystyle(D^{*\top}_{\mid W}P_{Z}D^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}\left(D^{*\top}_{\mid W}P_{Z}\widehat{\Omega}P_{Z}D^{*}_{\mid W}\right)(D^{*\top}_{\mid W}P_{Z}D^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:fwl_projection}}{=}

\displaystyle(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{\Omega}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:fwl_se_dod}}{=}

\displaystyle(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}\left(\sum_{g=1}^{G}\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\cdot r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}^{\top}\widehat{D}^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\right)(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}\cdot\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\cdot r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}^{\top}\widehat{D}^{*}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\cdot(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}\left\{(\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{\mid W}\widehat{D}^{*}_{\mid W})^{-1}\cdot\widehat{D}^{*\top}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:fwl_se_ddd}}{=}

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{N^{2}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{S_{ZD\mid W}^{2}}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\left(Z^{*\top}_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\cdot r_{{[g]}{\mid W}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{N^{2}}\cdot\dfrac{1}{S_{ZD\mid W}^{2}}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\left(\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}\cdot r_{i{\mid W}}\right)^{2}.

Proof of Lemma
S10
(
ii
)

Analogous to
\hat{\gamma}_{W,Z}
, let
\hat{\gamma}_{W,D}=\texttt{coef}(D_{i}^{*}\sim W_{i})
denote the coefficient vector of
W_{i}
from
\texttt{ols}(D_{i}^{*}\sim W_{i})
,
with

\displaystyle D_{i\mid W}^{*}=D_{i}^{*}-W_{i}^{\top}\hat{\gamma}_{W,D}.

(S45)

The first-order condition of
\texttt{ols}(Z_{i}^{*}\sim W_{i})
ensures

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}W_{i}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}=0.

(S46)

Equations (
S45
)–(
S46
) together ensure

\displaystyle S_{ZD\mid W}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}D_{i\mid W}^{*}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:fwl_disw}}{=}

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}D_{i}^{*}-\left(N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}W_{i}^{\top}\right)\hat{\gamma}_{W,D}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:fwl_1st_zw}}{=}

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid W}^{*}D_{i}^{*}\qquad\text{--- the first equivalent form}

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{Def. of $\hat{\gamma}_{W,Z}$}}{=}

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i}^{*}-\hat{\gamma}_{W,Z}^{\top}W_{i})D_{i}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i}^{*}D_{i}^{*}-\hat{\gamma}_{W,Z}^{\top}N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}W_{i}D_{i}^{*}.\quad\text{--- the second equivalent form}

The proof for
S_{ZY\mid W}
is identical and therefore omitted.
∎

S3.3
Lemmas under the IV setting

Recall from (
S1.2
)–(
S8
) that

\displaystyle Z=(Z_{11},\ldots,Z_{G,{n_{G}}})^{\top}=(Z_{[1]}^{\top},\ldots,Z_{[G]}^{\top})^{\top},

\displaystyle\dot{Z}_{[g]}=(Z_{i}-\bar{Z}_{g}:{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}),

\displaystyle S_{Z}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z})^{2},

\displaystyle S_{Z,g}=\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z}_{{c(i)}})^{2}=\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\dot{Z}_{[g]},

with
S_{Z,\textup{in}}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z}_{{c(i)}})^{2}=N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{Z,g}
.
Recall that

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\phi_{g}=n_{g}^{-2}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}\corr(Z_{i},Z_{i^{\prime}})\in[0,1],\quad\displaystyle\kappa_{N}=\E(S_{Z,\textup{in}})/\sigma^{2}_{Z},\quad\displaystyle c_{N}=N\var(\bar{Z})/\sigma^{2}_{Z}.\end{array}

Lemma
S11
below verifies the equivalent forms of
\phi_{g}
,
\kappa_{N}
, and
c_{N}
in (
11
).

Lemma S11
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
3
, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{g}=(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\var(\bar{Z}_{g})\in[0,1],

(S47)

\displaystyle\E(S_{Z})=\sigma^{2}_{Z}-\var(\bar{Z}),\qquad\E(S_{Z,g})=\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot n_{g}(1-\phi_{g}),

\displaystyle\E(S_{Z,\textup{in}})=N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\E(S_{Z,g})=\sigma^{2}_{Z}\left(1-N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}n_{g}\phi_{g}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa_{N}=(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\cdot N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\E(S_{Z,g})=1-N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}n_{g}\phi_{g},\qquad c_{N}=N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}n_{g}^{2}\phi_{g}.\qquad\qquad

(S48)

Proof of Lemma
S11
.

We verify below the expressions of
\phi_{g}
,
\E(S_{Z})
,
\E(S_{Z,g})
, and
c_{N}
.
The expressions of
\E(S_{Z,\textup{in}})
and
\kappa_{N}
then follow from
S_{Z,\textup{in}}=N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{Z,g}
by definition in (
S8
) and
\kappa_{N}=\E(S_{Z,\textup{in}})/\sigma^{2}_{Z}
by definition in (
4
).

Proof of (
S47
).

It follows from
\corr(Z_{i},Z_{i^{\prime}})\leq 1
that
\phi_{g}\leq 1
. In addition, the correlation matrix is positive semidefinite. This implies
\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}\corr(Z_{i},Z_{i^{\prime}})\geq 0
so that
\phi_{g}\geq 0
.

Under Assumptions
2
–
3
,
\corr(Z_{i},Z_{i^{\prime}})=(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\cov(Z_{i},Z_{i^{\prime}})
so that

\phi_{g}=(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\cdot n_{g}^{-2}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}\cov(Z_{i},Z_{i^{\prime}})=(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\cdot n_{g}^{-2}\var\left(\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}Z_{i}\right)=(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\var(\bar{Z}_{g}).

Proof of the expression of
\E(S_{Z})
.

Direct algebra ensures

\displaystyle S_{Z}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i}-e)^{2}-(\bar{Z}-e)^{2},

(S49)

where the expectations of the first and second terms are

\displaystyle\E\left\{N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i}-e)^{2}\right\}=\sigma^{2}_{Z},\quad\E\left\{(\bar{Z}-e)^{2}\right\}=\var(\bar{Z})=\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot N^{-1}\cdot c_{N}.

This implies the result.

Proof of the expression of
\E(S_{Z,g})
.

Applying (
S49
) to cluster
g
ensures

\displaystyle S_{Z,g}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:szt_decomp}}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-e)^{2}-n_{g}(\bar{Z}_{g}-e)^{2},

\displaystyle\E(S_{Z,g})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n_{g}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}-n_{g}\cdot\var(\bar{Z}_{g})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:phig_proof}}{=}

\displaystyle n_{g}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}-n_{g}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\phi_{g}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot n_{g}(1-\phi_{g}).

Proof of the expression of
c_{N}
.

Given
\phi_{g}=(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\var(\bar{Z}_{g})
from (
S47
), we have

\displaystyle c_{N}=(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\cdot N\var(\bar{Z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\cdot N^{-1}\var\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{Assumption~\ref{assm:cs}}}{=}

\displaystyle(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\cdot N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\var\left(\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}Z_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\sigma^{2}_{Z})^{-1}\cdot N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}n_{g}^{2}\cdot\var(\bar{Z}_{g})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:phig_proof}}{=}

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}n_{g}^{2}\phi_{g}.

∎

Recall from (
S17
) that
A_{i}=Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau_{\textup{c}}
.

Lemma S12
.

Assume Assumptions
2
–
3
.
Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}_{i}=Y_{i}(0)+1_{\{U_{i}=\textup{a}\}}\cdot\tau_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}Y_{i}(0)&\text{for compliers and never-takers};\\
Y_{i}(1)&\text{for always takers},\end{array}\right.

which is intuitively the potential outcome of unit
i
if
Z_{i}=0
.
Let
\widetilde{\mu}=\E(\widetilde{Y}_{i})
denote the common value of
\E(\widetilde{Y}_{i})
under Assumption
3
.
Let

\displaystyle S_{XZ,\textup{in}}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i},\quad S_{ZA,\textup{in}}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}.

Then

(i)

\E(Y_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\E(Y_{i}\mid Z_{i})=\widetilde{\mu}+Z_{i}\pi_{\textup{c}}\tau_{\textup{c}}
,

\mu_{Y}=\widetilde{\mu}+e\pi_{\textup{c}}\tau_{\textup{c}},\quad\mu_{ZY}=e(\widetilde{\mu}+\pi_{\textup{c}}\tau_{\textup{c}}),\quad\cov(Z_{i},Y_{i})=\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}}\tau_{\textup{c}}.

(ii)

\E(D_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\E(D_{i}\mid Z_{i})=\pi_{\textup{a}}+Z_{i}\pi_{\textup{c}}
,

\mu_{D}=\pi_{\textup{a}}+e\pi_{\textup{c}},\quad\mu_{ZD}=e(\pi_{\textup{a}}+\pi_{\textup{c}}),\quad\cov(Z_{i},D_{i})=\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}}
,

\E(\dot{D}_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\dot{Z}_{i}\pi_{\textup{c}}
,

\E(\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{D}_{i})=\pi_{\textup{c}}\cdot\E(\dot{Z}_{i}^{2}),\quad\E(S_{ZD,\textup{in}})=\pi_{\textup{c}}\cdot\E(S_{Z,\textup{in}})=\kappa_{N}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}}.

(iii)

\E(A_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\E(A_{i}\mid Z_{i})=\widetilde{\mu}-\pi_{\textup{a}}\tau_{\textup{c}}=\mu_{A}
,
\E(A_{{[g]}}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\E(A_{{[g]}})=1_{n_{g}}\mu_{A}
,

\cov(Z_{{[g]}},A_{{[g]}})=0
,
\cov(Z_{i},A_{i})=\E(\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{A}_{i})=\E(S_{ZA,\textup{in}})=0
.

(iv)

\cov(X_{i},Z_{i})=\E(\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i})=\E(S_{XZ,\textup{in}})=0_{p}
.

Proof of Lemma
S12
.

We verify below Lemma
S12
(
i
)–(
iv
), respectively.

Proof of Lemma
S12
(
i
).

Write

D_{i}=1_{\{U_{i}=\textup{a}\}}+Z_{i}\cdot 1_{\{U_{i}=\textup{c}\}}.

(S53)

Assumption
2
(
i
) ensures

\displaystyle Y_{i}=Y_{i}(0)+D_{i}\tau_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_cef_di}}{=}

\displaystyle Y_{i}(0)+\Big(1_{\{U_{i}=\textup{a}\}}+Z_{i}\cdot 1_{\{U_{i}=\textup{c}\}}\Big)\tau_{i}

(S54)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Y_{i}(0)+1_{\{U_{i}=\textup{a}\}}\tau_{i}+Z_{i}\cdot 1_{\{U_{i}=\textup{c}\}}\tau_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}_{i}+Z_{i}\cdot 1_{\{U_{i}=\textup{c}\}}\tau_{i}.

This, together with Assumption
2
(
ii
), implies Lemma
S12
(
i
) as follows:

\displaystyle\E(Y_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:yi_tyi}}{=}

\displaystyle\E(\widetilde{Y}_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})+Z_{i}\cdot\E(1_{\{U_{i}=\textup{c}\}}\tau_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{Assm.~\ref{assm:iv}\eqref{it:assm_iv_random}}}{=}

\displaystyle\E(\widetilde{Y}_{i})+Z_{i}\cdot\E(1_{\{U_{i}=\textup{c}\}}\tau_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}+Z_{i}\cdot\mathbb{P}(U_{i}=\textup{c})\cdot\E\left(\tau_{i}\mid U_{i}=\textup{c}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}+Z_{i}\pi_{\textup{c}}\tau_{\textup{c}}.

Proof of Lemma
S12
(
ii
).

The first two lines in Lemma
S12
(
ii
) follows from defining the potential outcomes as
Y_{i}^{\prime}(d)=d
for
d=0,1
with
Y_{i}^{\prime}=D_{i}
and
\tau_{\textup{c}}^{\prime}=1
.

It then follows from
\E(D_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\pi_{\textup{a}}+Z_{i}\pi_{\textup{c}}
that

\E(\bar{D}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=n_{g}^{-1}\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\E(D_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\pi_{\textup{a}}+\bar{Z}\pi_{\textup{c}},\quad\E(\dot{D}_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\dot{Z}_{i}\pi_{\textup{c}},

(S55)

which in turn implies

\E(\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{D}_{i})=\E\left\{\E(\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}\mid\dot{Z}_{[g]})\right\}=\E\left\{\dot{Z}_{i}\cdot\E(\dot{D}_{i}\mid\dot{Z}_{[g]})\right\}\overset{\eqref{eq:eddi_1}}{=}\E(\dot{Z}_{i}^{2}\pi_{\textup{c}})=\pi_{\textup{c}}\cdot\E(\dot{Z}_{i}^{2}).

Proof of Lemma
S12
(
iii
).

Lemma
S12
(
i
)–(
ii
) ensure

\displaystyle\E(A_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\E(Y_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})-\tau_{\textup{c}}\cdot\E(D_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\widetilde{\mu}-\pi_{\textup{a}}\tau_{\textup{c}},

which is independent of
Z_{{[g]}}
. This implies

\E(A_{{[g]}}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\mu_{A}1_{n_{g}},\quad\text{where}\ \ \mu_{A}=\E(A_{i})=\widetilde{\mu}-\pi_{\textup{a}}\tau_{\textup{c}},

Accordingly, by the law of iterated expectations,

\displaystyle\cov(Z_{{[g]}},A_{{[g]}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\Big\{\cov(Z_{{[g]}},A_{{[g]}}\mid Z_{{[g]}})\Big\}+\cov\Big\{\E(Z_{{[g]}}\mid Z_{{[g]}}),\E(A_{{[g]}}\mid Z_{{[g]}})\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0+\cov(Z_{{[g]}},\mu_{A}1_{n_{g}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\E(\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{A}_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\left\{\E(\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})\right\}=\E\left\{\dot{Z}_{i}\cdot\E(\dot{A}_{i}\mid Z_{{[g]}})\right\}=\E\left\{\dot{Z}_{i}\cdot\E(\dot{A}_{i})\right\}=0.

Proof of Lemma
S12
(
iv
).

The renewed Assumption
2
(
ii
) ensures
Z_{{[g]}}\perp\!\!\!\perp(X_{i}:{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}})
.
This ensures

\displaystyle\cov(X_{i},Z_{i})=0,\quad\E(\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i})=\E(\dot{X}_{i})\cdot\E(\dot{Z}_{i})=0,\quad\E(S_{XZ,\textup{in}})=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i})=0.

∎

S3.3.1
Asymptotics

Lemma S13
.

Assume Assumptions
1
–
3
. Let

B_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{i}-\mu_{A}\\
Z_{i}(A_{i}-\mu_{A})\\
\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad S_{ZD,g}=\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}.

Then

(i)

Z_{i}
,
D_{i}
,
Z_{i}D_{i}
,
\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}
are all uniformly integrable over
i\in\mathcal{I}
.

(ii)

If Assumption
5
holds, then

\begin{array}[]{lll}\bar{Z}=e+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\bar{D}=\mu_{D}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),&&\displaystyle\overline{ZD}\equiv N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i}D_{i}=\mu_{ZD}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\\
S_{ZD}=\E(S_{ZD})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),&&S_{ZD,\textup{in}}=\E(S_{ZD,\textup{in}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\kappa_{N}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\\
\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{ZD,g}^{2}=O(1).\end{array}

(iii)

If
\E(Y_{i}^{4})<\infty
holds, then

•

\E(A_{i}^{4})=O(1)
,
\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\dot{A}_{i}^{4})\leq 2^{4}\cdot\E(A_{i}^{4})=O(1)
;

•

\{\|B_{i}\|^{2}:i\in\mathcal{I}\}
is uniformly integrable with
\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|B_{i}\|^{4})=O(1).

Proof of Lemma
S13
.

We verify below Lemma
S13
(
i
)–(
iii
) one by one.

Proof of Lemma
S13
(
i
).

Note that

Z_{i},D_{i}\in\{0,1\},\quad|\dot{Z}_{i}|,|\dot{D}_{i}|\in[0,1].

(S56)

This ensures
Z_{i}
,
D_{i}
,
Z_{i}D_{i}
, and
\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}
are all bounded hence uniformly integrable.

Proof of Lemma
S13
(
ii
).

Note that

S_{ZD,\textup{in}}=\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z}_{{c(i)}})(D_{i}-\bar{D}_{{c(i)}})=\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}

is the sample mean of
\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}
.
Under Assumptions
1
–
3
, Lemma
S13
(
i
) ensures that
Z_{i},\,D_{i},\,Z_{i}D_{i},\,\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}
are all uniformly integrable, and therefore satisfy the conditions required by Lemma
S1
(
i
) if we further require Assumption
5
.
Given

•

\E(\bar{Z})=e
,
\E(\bar{D})=\mu_{D}
,
\E(\overline{ZD})=\E(Z_{i}D_{i})=\mu_{ZD}
under Assumption
3
,

•

\E(S_{ZD,\textup{in}})=\kappa_{N}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}}
under Assumptions
2
–
3
by Lemma
S12
(
ii
),

applying Lemma
S1
(
i
) to
Z_{i},\,D_{i},\,Z_{i}D_{i},\,\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{D}_{i},
respectively, ensures

\displaystyle\bar{Z}=\E(\bar{Z})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=e+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\bar{D}=\E(\bar{D})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\mu_{D}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle\overline{ZD}=\E(\overline{ZD})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\mu_{ZD}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

\displaystyle S_{ZD,\textup{in}}=\E(S_{ZD,\textup{in}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\kappa_{N}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

This also implies that

\displaystyle S_{ZD}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z})(D_{i}-\bar{D})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i}D_{i}-\bar{Z}\bar{D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{ZD}-e\mu_{D}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cov(Z_{i},D_{i})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where
\cov(Z_{i},D_{i})=\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}}
under Assumptions
2
–
3
by Lemma
S12
(
ii
).

Lastly, (
S56
) ensures
S_{ZD,g}\leq n_{g}
so that

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{ZD,g}^{2}\leq N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}n_{g}^{2},

which is
O(1)
under Assumption
5
.

Proof of Lemma
S13
(
iii
).

By Lemma
S5
,

\begin{array}[]{rcccl}A_{i}^{4}=(Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau_{\textup{c}})^{4}&\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:bound}\eqref{it:lem_bound_1}}}{\leq}&2^{4-1}\left(Y_{i}^{4}+D_{i}^{4}\tau_{\textup{c}}^{4}\right)&\leq&2^{4-1}\left(Y_{i}^{4}+\tau_{\textup{c}}^{4}\right),\\
\E(\dot{A}_{i}^{4})&\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:bound}\eqref{it:lem_bound_2}}}{\leq}&2^{4}\cdot\E(A_{i}^{4})&\leq&2^{4}\cdot 2^{4-1}\big\{\E(Y_{i}^{4})+\tau_{\textup{c}}^{4}\big\}.\end{array}

(S57)

This implies
\E(A_{i}^{4})=O(1)
and
\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\dot{A}_{i}^{4})=O(1)
provided
\E(Y_{i}^{4})=O(1)
.

In addition, given
Z_{i},\dot{Z}_{i}^{2}\in[0,1]
from (
S56
),

\displaystyle\|B_{i}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(A_{i}-\mu_{A})^{2}+Z_{i}(A_{i}-\mu_{A})^{2}+(\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{A}_{i})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left\{(A_{i}-\mu_{A})^{2}+\dot{A}_{i}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\text{Cauchy--Schwarz}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2\left(2A_{i}^{2}+2\mu_{A}^{2}+\dot{A}_{i}^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\|B_{i}\|^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{2}\left(2A_{i}^{2}+2\mu_{A}^{2}+\dot{A}_{i}^{2}\right)^{2}\overset{\textup{\text{Cauchy--Schwarz}}}{\leq}2^{2}\cdot 3\left(4A_{i}^{4}+4\mu_{A}^{4}+\dot{A}_{i}^{4}\right).

Combining this with (
S57
) implies

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|B_{i}\|^{4})\leq 12\cdot\left\{4\E(A_{i}^{4})+4\mu_{A}^{4}+\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\dot{A}_{i}^{4})\right\}=O(1).

This implies
\|B_{i}\|^{2}
is uniformly integrable by Lemma
S7
(
iv
).
∎

Lemma S14
.

Recall from (
S20
) that
R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}=A_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\gamma_{X,A}
, where
\gamma_{X,A}=\{\E(S_{X,\textup{in}})\}^{-1}\E(S_{XA,\textup{in}})
.
Let

\begin{array}[]{l}B_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\dot{Z}_{i}\\
\dot{X}_{i}\end{pmatrix}R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}=\begin{pmatrix}\dot{Z}_{i}\\
\dot{X}_{i}\end{pmatrix}(\dot{A}_{i}-\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A}),\\
S_{XZ,g}=\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i},\quad S_{XD,g}=\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i},\quad S_{X,g}=\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{X}^{\top}_{i},\\
S_{ZR,g}=\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{Z}_{i}R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}},\quad S_{XR,g}=\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{X}_{i}R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}.\end{array}

Under Assumption
S2
(
i
)–(
ii
) and (
iv
),

(i)

\kappa_{N}>0
.

(ii)

Let
X_{ik}
denote the
k
th element of
X_{i}
for
k=1,\ldots,p
.
Under Assumption
S2
(
iv
),
\dot{X}_{i}
,
\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i}
,
\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}
,
\dot{X}_{ik}\dot{X}_{ik^{\prime}}\ (k,k^{\prime}=1,\ldots,p)
,
\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}
, and
\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|^{2}
are all uniformly integrable over
i\in\mathcal{I}
, with

\begin{array}[]{l}\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}\|)=O(1),\\
\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(|\dot{X}_{ik}\dot{X}_{ik^{\prime}}|)=O(1)\quad\forall k,k^{\prime}=1,\ldots,p,\\
\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|)=O(1),\quad\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|^{2+2q})=O(1)\quad\text{for}\ \ \ q=\dfrac{s-2}{s+2}>0\end{array}

as
N\to\infty
, where
s\in(2,\infty)
is the constant in Assumption
S2
(
iv
) such that
\E(\|X_{i}\|^{2s})<\infty
.

(iii)

S_{XZ,\textup{in}}=\E(S_{XZ,\textup{in}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
S_{XD,\textup{in}}=\E(S_{XD,\textup{in}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

S_{XA,\textup{in}}=\E(S_{XA,\textup{in}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
S_{X,\textup{in}}=\E(S_{X,\textup{in}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

where
\E(S_{XZ,\textup{in}})=0
, and
\E(S_{XD,\textup{in}})
,
\E(S_{XA,\textup{in}})
, and
\E(S_{X,\textup{in}})
are componentwise
O(1)
.

(iv)

\gamma_{X,A}
is componentwise
O(1)
;

\{\|B_{i}\|^{2}:i\in\mathcal{I}\}
is uniformly integrable with
\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|B_{i}\|^{2+2q})=O(1)
.

(v)

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}\|^{4}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

(vi)

N^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{XZ,g}S_{XZ,g}^{\top}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
N^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{XD,g}S_{XD,g}^{\top}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

N^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|S_{X,g}\|_{2}^{2}\leq N^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|S_{X,g}\|_{\textsc{f}}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
N^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{XR,g}S_{XR,g}^{\top}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Proof of Lemma
S14
.

We verify below Lemma
S14
(
i
)–(
vi
), respectively.

Proof of Lemma
S14
(
i
).

If
\kappa_{N}=0
, then
\dot{Z}_{i}=0
for all
i
, so that

\displaystyle(1,0_{p}^{\top})\Omega_{N,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0_{p}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:omgnlsx_omgnfex_def}}{=}

\displaystyle(1,0_{p}^{\top})\cdot N^{-1}\cov\left\{\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\begin{pmatrix}\dot{Z}_{i}\\
\dot{X}_{i}\end{pmatrix}R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\right\}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0_{p}^{\top}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{-1}\cov\left\{\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(1,0_{p}^{\top})\begin{pmatrix}\dot{Z}_{i}\\
\dot{X}_{i}\end{pmatrix}R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{-1}\cov\left(\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{Z}_{i}R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\right)=0,

which contradicts with
\lambda_{\min}(\Omega_{N,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}})>0
under Assumption
S2
(
iv
).

Proof of Lemma
S14
(
ii
).

Assumption
S2
(
iv
) ensures
\E(\|X_{i}\|^{2s})=O(1)
, so that

\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2s})\leq 2^{2s}\cdot\E(\|X_{i}\|^{2s})=O(1)

(S58)

by Lemma
S5
(
ii
).
This implies uniform integrability of
\dot{X}_{i}
over
i\in\mathcal{I}
by Lemma
S7
(
iv
), and
together with

\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i}\|,\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}\|\leq\|\dot{X}_{i}\|,\quad|\dot{X}_{ik}\dot{X}_{ik^{\prime}}|\leq 2^{-1}\left(\dot{X}_{ik}^{2}+\dot{X}_{ik^{\prime}}^{2}\right)\leq 2^{-1}\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2},

(S59)

implies uniform integrability of
\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i}
,
\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}
, and
|\dot{X}_{ik}\dot{X}_{ik^{\prime}}|
.
Also, it follows from

\{\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|)\}^{2}\leq\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2})\leq\{\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2s})\}^{1/s}

by Lemma
S5
(
iii
) that

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}\|)\leq\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|)\overset{\textup{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:bound}\eqref{it:lem_bound_3}}}}{\leq}\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\left\{\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2s})\right\}^{\frac{1}{2s}}\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_fex_XX_2}}{=}O(1),\\
\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(|\dot{X}_{ik}\dot{X}_{ik^{\prime}}|)\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_fex_XX_1}}{\leq}\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2})\overset{\textup{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:bound}\eqref{it:lem_bound_3}}}}{\leq}\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\left\{\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2s})\right\}^{1/s}\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_fex_XX_2}}{=}O(1).\end{array}

In addition, let

q=\dfrac{s-2}{s+2}\in(0,1),\quad g=\dfrac{s+2}{2}\in(1,\infty),\quad h=\dfrac{s+2}{s}\in(1,\infty)

with

2+2q=\dfrac{4s}{s+2},\quad 1/g+1/h=1,\quad(2+2q)g=2s,\quad(2+2q)h=4.

(S60)

Given

\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|=\sqrt{\dot{A}_{i}\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}}=\|\dot{X}_{i}\|\cdot|\dot{A}_{i}|,

Holder’s inequality in Lemma
S4
(
i
) implies

\displaystyle\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|^{2+2q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\left(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2+2q}\cdot|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2+2q}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\E\left\{(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2+2q})^{g}\right\}\right]^{1/g}\cdot\left[\E\left\{(|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2+2q})^{h}\right\}\right]^{1/h}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:holder_gh}}{=}

\displaystyle\left\{\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2s})\right\}^{1/g}\cdot\left\{\E(\dot{A}_{i}^{4})\right\}^{1/h}.

This, together with
\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2s})=O(1)
from (
S58
) and
\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{A}_{i}\|^{4})=O(1)
from
Lemma
S13
(
iii
), implies

\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|^{2+2q})\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_fex_XX_2}+\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:asym_nox}\eqref{it:lem_asym_nox_AB}}}{=}O(1)

(S61)

under Assumption
S2
(
ii
) and (
iv
),
so that both
\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}
and
\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|^{2}
are uniformly integrable over
i\in\mathcal{I}
by Lemma
S7
(
iv
).

Lastly, Lemma
S5
(
iii
) ensures
\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|)\leq\{\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|^{2+2q})\}^{1/(2+2q)}
, so that

\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|)\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:bound}\eqref{it:lem_bound_3}}}{\leq}\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\left\{\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|^{2+2q})\right\}^{1/(2+2q)}\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_dxda_2q}}{=}O(1)

by (
S61
).

Proof of Lemma
S14
(
iii
).

Lemma
S14
(
ii
) ensures that, under Assumption
S2
(
iv
),

\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i},\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i},\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}
, and all elements in
\dot{X}_{i}\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}
are all uniformly integrable over
i\in\mathcal{I}
, and therefore satisfy the conditions required by Lemma
S1
(
i
).
Applying Lemma
S1
(
i
) to
\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i},\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i},\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}
, and all elements in
\dot{X}_{i}\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}
implies

\begin{array}[]{rclcl}S_{XZ,\textup{in}}&=&N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{Z}_{i}&=&\E(S_{XZ,\textup{in}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\\
S_{XD,\textup{in}}&=&N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}&=&\E(S_{XD,\textup{in}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\\
S_{XA,\textup{in}}&=&N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}&=&\E(S_{XA,\textup{in}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\\
S_{X,\textup{in}}&=&N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}&=&\E(S_{X,\textup{in}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).\end{array}

(S62)

Lemma
S12
(
iv
) implies
\E(S_{XZ,\textup{in}})=0
, so the first row in (
S62
) implies
S_{XZ,\textup{in}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Let
S_{X,\textup{in}}[k,k^{\prime}]
denote the
(k,k^{\prime})
th element of
S_{X,\textup{in}}
.
The triangle inequality of norms implies

\begin{array}[]{rclcl}\|S_{XD,\textup{in}}\|&=&\displaystyle\left\|N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}\right\|&\leq&\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}\|,\\
\|S_{XA,\textup{in}}\|&=&\displaystyle\left\|N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\right\|&\leq&\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|,\\
|S_{X,\textup{in}}[k,k^{\prime}]|&=&\displaystyle\left|N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\dot{X}_{ik}\dot{X}_{ik^{\prime}}\right|&\leq&\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\dot{X}_{ik}\dot{X}_{ik^{\prime}}|.\end{array}

(S63)

Combining this with

\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}\|)=O(1),\quad\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|)=O(1),\quad\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(|\dot{X}_{ik}\dot{X}_{ik^{\prime}}|)=O(1)

as
N\to\infty
by Lemma
S14
(
ii
) implies

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\E(\|S_{XD,\textup{in}}\|)&\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_S_triangle}}{\leq}&\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{D}_{i}\|)\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:fex}\eqref{it:lem_fex_uni_X}}}{=}O(1),\\
\E(\|S_{XA,\textup{in}}\|)&\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_S_triangle}}{\leq}&\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|)\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:fex}\eqref{it:lem_fex_uni_X}}}{=}O(1),\\
\E(|S_{X,\textup{in}}[k,k^{\prime}]|)&\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_S_triangle}}{\leq}&\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E\left(\left|\dot{X}_{ik}\dot{X}_{ik^{\prime}}\right|\right)\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:fex}\eqref{it:lem_fex_uni_X}}}{=}O(1)\end{array}

(S64)

as
N\to\infty
.
Equation (
S64
) implies
\E(S_{XD,\textup{in}})
,
\E(S_{XA,\textup{in}})
, and
\E(S_{X,\textup{in}})
are all componentwise
O(1)
, so that
S_{XD,\textup{in}}
,
S_{XY,\textup{in}}
, and
S_{X,\textup{in}}
are all componentwise
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
from (
S62
).

Proof of Lemma
S14
(
iv
).

Lemma
S14
(
iii
) ensures
\E(S_{XA,\textup{in}})=O(1)
.
This, together with
\lambda_{\min}\{\E(S_{X,\textup{in}})\}\geq\lambda>0
under Assumption
S2
(
iv
), ensures

\gamma_{X,A}\overset{\eqref{eq:rrifex_def}}{=}\{\E(S_{X,\textup{in}})\}^{-1}\E(S_{XA,\textup{in}})=O(1).

(S65)

In addition, given

B_{i}=\begin{pmatrix}\dot{Z}_{i}\\
\dot{X}_{i}\end{pmatrix}(\dot{A}_{i}-\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A})=\begin{pmatrix}\dot{Z}_{i}(\dot{A}_{i}-\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A})\\
\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}-\dot{X}_{i}\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A}\end{pmatrix}
,
it follows from

\displaystyle|\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A}|\leq\|\dot{X}_{i}\|\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|,

\displaystyle\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A}\|=\|\dot{X}_{i}\|\cdot|\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A}|\leq\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|,

\displaystyle\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|=\|\dot{X}_{i}\|\cdot|\dot{A}_{i}|

that

\begin{array}[]{rcccl}\dot{Z}_{i}^{2}(\dot{A}_{i}-\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A})^{2}&\leq&2(|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2}+|\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A}|^{2})&\leq&2(|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2}),\\
\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}-\dot{X}_{i}\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2}&\leq&2\left(\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}\|^{2}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2}\right)&\leq&2\left(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2}\cdot|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{4}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2}\right),\end{array}

with

\displaystyle\|B_{i}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dot{Z}_{i}^{2}(\dot{A}_{i}-\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A})^{2}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}-\dot{X}_{i}\dot{X}^{\top}_{i}\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2}

(S66)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left(|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2}\cdot|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{4}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2}\right).

Recall from Lemma
S14
(
ii
) that
q=\dfrac{s-2}{s+2}\in(0,1)
with
1+q=\dfrac{2s}{s+2}
.
Lemma
S5
(
i
) ensures

\displaystyle\dfrac{\|B_{i}\|^{2+2q}}{2^{1+q}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\dfrac{\|B_{i}\|^{2}}{2}\right)^{1+q}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:uni_bi_1}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\left(|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2}\cdot|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{4}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2}\right)^{1+q}

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:bound}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 4^{q}\left(|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2+2q}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2+2q}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2+2q}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2+2q}\cdot|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2+2q}+\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{4+4q}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2+2q}\right),

so that, after dividing both sides by
4^{q}
and taking supreme over expectations,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\dfrac{\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|B_{i}\|^{2+2q})}{2^{1+q}\cdot 4^{q}}&\leq&\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2+2q})+\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2+2q}\cdot\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2+2q})\\
&&+\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E\left(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2+2q}\cdot|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2+2q}\right)+\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2+2q}\cdot\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{4+4q}).\end{array}

(S67)

Note that
q<1
and
s>2
, so that

2+2q<4<2s,\quad 4+4q=4\cdot\dfrac{2s}{s+2}<2s.

By Lemma
S4
(
ii
) and Lemma
S5
(
iii
),

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2+2q})&\leq&\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(|\dot{A}_{i}|^{4})\right\}^{(2+2q)/4}\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:asym_nox}\eqref{it:lem_asym_nox_AB}}}{=}O(1),\\
\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2+2q})&\leq&\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(|\dot{X}_{i}|^{2s})\right\}^{(2+2q)/2s}\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_fex_XX_2}}{=}O(1),\\
\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(|\dot{X}_{i}|^{4+4q})&\leq&\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(|\dot{X}_{i}|^{2s})\right\}^{(4+4q)/2s}\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_fex_XX_2}}{=}O(1).\end{array}

(S68)

In addition, (
S65
) and Lemma
S14
(
ii
) imply

\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{2+2q}=O(1),\quad\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E\left(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2+2q}\cdot|\dot{A}_{i}|^{2+2q}\right)=O(1).

(S69)

Plugging (
S68
) and (
S69
) into (
S67
) implies

\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(\|B_{i}\|^{2+2q})=O(1),

so that
\|B_{i}\|^{2}
is uniformly integrable over
i\in\mathcal{I}
by Lemma
S7
(
iv
).

Proof of Lemma
S14
(
v
).

We verify below the results for
\dot{X}_{[g]}
,
\dot{Y}_{[g]}
, and
R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}
, respectively.

Proof of
N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Properties of norms ensure

\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}\leq\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{\textsc{f}};

(S70)

c.f. (
S1
).
Also, it follows from

\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{\textsc{f}}^{2}=\|(\dot{X}_{g1},\dot{X}_{g2},\ldots,\dot{X}_{g,n_{g}})^{\top}\|_{\textsc{f}}^{2}=\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2}
that

\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{\textsc{f}}^{4}=\left(\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{2}\right)^{2}\overset{\textup{\text{Cauchy--Schwarz}}}{\leq}n_{g}\left(\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{4}\right)

(S71)

by Cauchy–Schwarz inequality.
Lemma
S5
(
ii
) further ensures that, under Assumption
3
,

\E\left(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{4}\right)\leq 2^{4}\cdot\E\left(\|X_{i}\|^{4}\right).

(S72)

Combining (
S70
)–(
S72
) implies

\displaystyle\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:oop_X_1}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{\textsc{f}}^{4}\overset{\eqref{eq:oop_X_2}}{\leq}n_{g}\left(\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{4}\right),

\displaystyle\E\left(\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle n_{g}\left\{\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\E\left(\|\dot{X}_{i}\|^{4}\right)\right\}

(S73)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:oop_X_3}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 16\cdot n_{g}\left\{\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\E(\|X_{i}\|^{4})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16\cdot n_{g}^{2}\cdot\E(\|X_{i}\|^{4}),

so that

\displaystyle\E\left(N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}\right)=N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\E\left(\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}\right)\overset{\eqref{eq:oop_X_4}}{\leq}16\cdot\E(\|X_{i}\|^{4})\cdot N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}n_{g}^{2}.

(S74)

Assumption
S2
(
i
) and (
iv
) ensure

\E(\|X_{i}\|^{4})\leq\left\{\E(\|X_{i}\|^{2s})\right\}^{\frac{4}{2s}}<\infty,\quad\limsup_{N\to\infty}N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}n_{g}^{2}<\infty

in (
S74
) so that

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\E\left(N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}\right)\overset{\eqref{eq:oop_X_5}}{<}\infty.

It then follows from Lemma
S6
(
iib
) that

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:basic}}}{=}O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(S75)

Proof of
N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}\|^{4}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Replacing
\dot{X}_{[g]}
by
\dot{Y}_{[g]}
in the above proof with

\displaystyle\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{2}=\lambda_{\max}\left(\dot{Y}_{[g]}^{\top}\dot{Y}_{[g]}\right)=\dot{Y}_{[g]}^{\top}\dot{Y}_{[g]}=\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}\|^{2},\quad\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}=\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}\|^{4}

ensures

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}\|^{4}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(S76)

under Assumption
S2
(
i
) and (
ii
).

Proof of
N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Given
|\dot{D}_{i}|\leq 1
, we have

\|\dot{D}_{[g]}\|^{2}=\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{D}_{i}^{2}\leq n_{g},\quad\|\dot{D}_{[g]}\|^{4}\leq n_{g}^{2},

so that

N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{D}_{[g]}\|^{4}\leq\limsup_{N\to\infty}N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}n_{g}^{2}=O(1).

(S77)

under Assumption
S2
(
i
).
In addition, Lemma
S14
(
iv
) ensures

\gamma_{X,A}=O(1),\quad\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{4}=O(1).

(S78)

Lemma
S8
(
i
) further implies

\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\gamma_{X,A}\|^{4}\leq\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{4}.

(S79)

It then follows from Lemma
S5
(
i
)
that

\displaystyle\|R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}-\dot{D}_{[g]}\tau_{\textup{c}}-\dot{X}_{[g]}\gamma_{X,A}\|^{4}

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:bound}\eqref{it:lem_bound_1}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 3^{4-1}\cdot\left(\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}\|^{4}+\|\dot{D}_{[g]}\|^{4}\cdot\tau_{\textup{c}}^{4}+\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\gamma_{X,A}\|^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{\eqref{eq:bound_S2_gxa2}}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 3^{4-1}\cdot\left(\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}\|^{4}+\|\dot{D}_{[g]}\|^{4}\cdot\tau_{\textup{c}}^{4}+\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}\cdot\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{4}\right),

so that

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3^{4-1}\left(N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{Y}_{[g]}\|^{4}+\tau_{\textup{c}}^{4}\cdot N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{D}_{[g]}\|^{4}+\|\gamma_{X,A}\|^{4}\cdot N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_S2_X}-\eqref{eq:bound_gxa_3}}{=}

\displaystyle O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

from (
S75
)–(
S78
).

Proof of Lemma
S14
(
vi
).

We verify below that
N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{XR,g}S_{XR,g}^{\top}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. The proof of the rest is similar hence omitted.

Let
S_{XR,g}[k]
denote the
k
th element of
S_{XR,g}=\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{X}_{i}R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}=\dot{X}_{[g]}^{\top}R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}
:

S_{XR,g}[k]=\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{X}_{ik}R_{i,{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}.

Lemma
S8
(
i
) implies

\|S_{XR,g}\|=\|\dot{X}_{[g]}^{\top}R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\|\leq\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}\cdot\|R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\|

(S80)

so that

\displaystyle\big|S_{XR,g}[k]\cdot S_{XR,g}[k^{\prime}]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-1}\left\{\big(S_{XR,g}[k]\big)^{2}+\big(S_{XR,g}[k^{\prime}]\big)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-1}\|S_{XR,g}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:bound_Sxrg}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2^{-1}\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4^{-1}\left(\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}+\|R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4}\right).

Therefore, the
(k,k^{\prime})
th element of
N^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{XR,g}S_{XR,g}^{\top}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{XR,g}[k]\cdot S_{XR,g}[k^{\prime}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\big|S_{XR,g}[k]\cdot S_{XR,g}[k^{\prime}]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4^{-1}\left(N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|\dot{X}_{[g]}\|_{2}^{4}+N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\|R_{{[g]},{\textup{2sfe}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:fex}\eqref{it:lem_fex_X_Y_R}}}{=}

\displaystyle O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

by Lemma
S14
(
v
).
This implies
N^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}S_{XR,g}S_{XR,g}^{\top}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
∎

S3.3.2
Lemmas under Assumption
6

Lemma S15
.

Under Assumption
6
,

\displaystyle A_{i}=\alpha_{{c(i)}}+\epsilon_{i},\quad A_{{[g]}}=1_{n_{g}}\alpha_{g}+\epsilon_{{[g]}},\quad\dot{A}_{{[g]}}=\dot{\epsilon}_{[g]}=P_{n_{g}}\epsilon_{{[g]}},

(S81)

with

\cov(A_{{[g]}})=\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}I_{n_{g}}+\sigma^{2}_{\alpha}1_{n_{g}}1_{n_{g}}^{\top},\quad\cov(\dot{A}_{{[g]}})=\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}(I_{n_{g}}-n_{g}^{-1}1_{n_{g}}1_{n_{g}}^{\top}).

Proof of Lemma
S15
.

The functional form of Assumption
6
ensures

Y_{i}=D_{i}\tau+\alpha_{{c(i)}}+\epsilon_{i},\quad\tau_{i}=Y_{i}(1)-Y_{i}(0)=\tau\quad\text{for}\ \ i\in\mathcal{I},\quad\tau_{\textup{c}}=\tau.

This implies (
S81
) as

A_{i}=Y_{i}-D_{i}\tau_{\textup{c}}=D_{i}\tau+\alpha_{{c(i)}}+\epsilon_{i}-D_{i}\tau=\alpha_{{c(i)}}+\epsilon_{i},

with

A_{{[g]}}=1_{n_{g}}\alpha_{g}+\epsilon_{{[g]}},\quad\dot{A}_{{[g]}}=P_{n_{g}}A_{{[g]}}=P_{n_{g}}(1_{n_{g}}\alpha_{g}+\epsilon_{{[g]}})=P_{n_{g}}\epsilon_{{[g]}}.

Expression of
\cov(A_{{[g]}})
.

Given
A_{{[g]}}=1_{n_{g}}\alpha_{g}+\epsilon_{{[g]}}
from (
S81
), we have

\displaystyle\E(A_{{[g]}}\mid\alpha_{g})=1_{n_{g}}\alpha_{g},\quad\cov(A_{{[g]}}\mid\alpha_{g})=\cov(\epsilon_{{[g]}}\mid\alpha_{g})=\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}I_{n_{g}},

(S82)

with

\displaystyle\cov(A_{{[g]}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\big\{\cov(A_{{[g]}}\mid\alpha_{g})\big\}+\cov\big\{\E(A_{{[g]}}\mid\alpha_{g})\big\}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_eff_4}}{=}

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}I_{n_{g}}+\cov(1_{n_{g}}\alpha_{g})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}I_{n_{g}}+\sigma^{2}_{\alpha}1_{n_{g}}1_{n_{g}}^{\top}.

Expression of
\cov(\dot{A}_{{[g]}})
.

(
S81
) and
\cov(\epsilon_{{[g]}})=\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}I_{n_{g}}
under Assumption
6
together ensure

\displaystyle\cov(\dot{A}_{{[g]}})\overset{\eqref{eq:aai_model}}{=}\cov(P_{n_{g}}\epsilon_{{[g]}})=P_{n_{g}}\cov(\epsilon_{{[g]}})P_{n_{g}}=\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}P_{n_{g}}=\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}(I_{n_{g}}-n_{g}^{-1}J_{n_{g}}).

∎

Lemma S16
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
3
and
6
,

\displaystyle\var\left\{\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z}_{g})(A_{i}-\bar{A}_{g})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\cdot\E(S_{Z,g})=\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\cdot n_{g}(1-\phi_{g}),

(S83)

\displaystyle\var\left\{\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-e)(A_{i}-\mu_{A})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\left(\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\cdot n_{g}+\sigma^{2}_{\alpha}\cdot n_{g}^{2}\phi_{g}\right),

(S84)

where
\phi_{g}=n_{g}^{-2}\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}\corr(Z_{i},Z_{i^{\prime}})
as defined in (
10
).

Proof of Lemma
S16
.

We verify below (
S83
) and (
S84
), respectively.

Proof of (
S83
).

Lemma
S15
ensures
\dot{A}_{{[g]}}=\dot{\epsilon}_{[g]}

so that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z}_{g})(A_{i}-\bar{A}_{g})=\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\dot{Z}_{i}\dot{A}_{i}=\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\dot{A}_{{[g]}}\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:A}}}{=}\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\dot{\epsilon}_{[g]}\overset{\eqref{eq:apb}}{=}\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\epsilon_{{[g]}}.

(S85)

In addition, Assumptions
2
and
6
ensure

\displaystyle Z_{{[g]}}\perp\!\!\!\perp\epsilon_{{[g]}},\quad\E(\epsilon_{{[g]}})=0_{n_{g}},\quad\E(\epsilon_{{[g]}}\epsilon_{{[g]}}^{\top})=\cov(\epsilon_{{[g]}})=\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}I_{n_{g}},\quad\E(\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\epsilon_{{[g]}})=0.

(S86)

Equations (
S85
)–(
S86
) together imply

\displaystyle\var\left\{\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-\bar{Z}_{g})(A_{i}-\bar{A}_{g})\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_eff_2}}{=}

\displaystyle\var(\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\epsilon_{{[g]}})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_eff_3}}{=}

\displaystyle\E\left\{\left(\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\epsilon_{{[g]}}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\left(\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\epsilon_{{[g]}}\epsilon_{{[g]}}^{\top}\dot{Z}_{[g]}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\Big\{\E\left(\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\epsilon_{{[g]}}\epsilon_{{[g]}}^{\top}\dot{Z}_{[g]}\mid Z_{{[g]}}\right)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\Big\{\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\cdot\E\left(\epsilon_{{[g]}}\epsilon_{{[g]}}^{\top}\mid Z_{{[g]}}\right)\cdot\dot{Z}_{[g]}\Big\}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_eff_3}}{=}

\displaystyle\E\Big\{\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\cdot\E(\epsilon_{{[g]}}\epsilon_{{[g]}}^{\top})\cdot\dot{Z}_{[g]}\Big\}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_eff_3}}{=}

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\cdot\E(\dot{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\dot{Z}_{[g]})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:szg}}{=}

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\cdot\E(S_{Z,g})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:Z}}}{=}

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot n_{g}(1-\phi_{g}),

where the last equality follows from
\E(S_{Z,g})=\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot n_{g}(1-\phi_{g})

by Lemma
S11
,

Proof of (
S84
).

Let
\check{Z}_{[g]}=Z_{{[g]}}-1_{n_{g}}e
and
\check{A}_{[g]}=A_{{[g]}}-1_{n_{g}}\mu_{A}
to write

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-e)(A_{i}-\mu_{A})=\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\check{A}_{[g]}.

(S87)

Lemma
S15
ensures
A_{{[g]}}\perp\!\!\!\perp Z_{{[g]}}
so that

\check{A}_{[g]}\perp\!\!\!\perp Z_{{[g]}},\quad\E(\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\check{A}_{[g]}\mid Z_{{[g]}})=\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\cdot\E(\check{A}_{[g]})=0.

(S88)

In addition, the definition of
\check{Z}_{[g]}
ensures

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\E\left(\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\check{Z}_{[g]}\right)&=&\E\left\{\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-e)^{2}\right\}\\
&=&n_{g}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z},\\
\E\left(\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\cdot 1_{n_{g}}1_{n_{g}}^{\top}\cdot\check{Z}_{[g]}\right)&=&\E\left(1_{n_{g}}^{\top}\cdot\check{Z}_{[g]}\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\cdot 1_{n_{g}}\right)\\
&=&1_{n_{g}}^{\top}\cdot\cov(Z_{{[g]}})\cdot 1_{n_{g}}\\
&=&\sum_{i,i^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}\cov(Z_{i},Z_{i^{\prime}})\\
&=&\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot n_{g}^{2}\phi_{g}.\end{array}

(S89)

Equations (
S88
)–(
S89
) together ensure

\displaystyle\var\left\{\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-e)(A_{i}-\mu_{A})\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:nzg}}{=}

\displaystyle\var(\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\check{A}_{[g]})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\E\Big\{\var\left(\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\check{A}_{[g]}\mid Z_{{[g]}}\right)\Big\}+\var\Big\{\E\left(\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\check{A}_{[g]}\mid Z_{{[g]}}\right)\Big\}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_eff_5}}{=}

\displaystyle\E\Big\{\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\cdot\cov(\check{A}_{[g]}\mid Z_{{[g]}})\cdot\check{Z}_{[g]}\Big\}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_eff_5}}{=}

\displaystyle\E\Big\{\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\cdot\cov(A_{{[g]}})\cdot\check{Z}_{[g]}\Big\}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{lem:A}}}{=}

\displaystyle\E\Big\{\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\cdot\left(\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}I_{n_{g}}+\sigma^{2}_{\alpha}1_{n_{g}}1_{n_{g}}^{\top}\right)\cdot\check{Z}_{[g]}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\cdot\E\left(\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\check{Z}_{[g]}\right)+\sigma^{2}_{\alpha}\cdot\E\left(\check{Z}_{[g]}^{\top}\cdot 1_{n_{g}}1_{n_{g}}^{\top}\cdot\check{Z}_{[g]}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:lem_eff_6}}{=}

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\epsilon}\cdot n_{g}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}+\sigma^{2}_{\alpha}\cdot n_{g}^{2}\phi_{g}\cdot\sigma^{2}_{Z}.

∎

.
This ensures
\Gamma_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}=\Gamma_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}
has full rank.

S4.1.2.2
Proof of Lemma
S2
(
iv
).

Recall from (
S30
) that

\Omega_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}=N^{-1}\cov\left(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}R_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\right)=N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\cov\left(\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}R_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\right)

under Assumptions
1
–
3
.
Under (
•
‣
S4.1
) and Assumption
3
, let

\Psi_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}=\E(w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}^{\top})

(S100)

denote the common value of
\E(w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}v_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}^{\top})
across
i\in\mathcal{I}
, which is independent of
N
.
The analogs of
\Psi_{N}
and
\Omega_{N}
in Lemma
S2
(
iv
), as defined in (
S2.1
), are

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\Psi_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\overset{\eqref{eq:hansen_def}+\eqref{eq:model_lsx}}{=}N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\E(w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}^{\top})\overset{\textup{Assumption~\ref{assm:im}}}{=}\E(w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}^{\top})\overset{\eqref{eq:psilsx_def}}{=}\Psi_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}},\\
\Omega_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}^{*}\overset{\eqref{eq:hansen_def}+\eqref{eq:model_lsx}}{=}N^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}\cov\left(\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}R_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\right)\overset{\eqref{eq:omgnlsx_omgnfex_def}}{=}\Omega_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}},\end{array}

(S101)

respectively,
so that
Lemma
S2
(
iv
) holds if

\lambda_{\min}(\Psi_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}})\overset{\eqref{eq:psinlsx}}{=}\lambda_{\min}(\Psi_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}})\geq\lambda,\quad\lambda_{\min}(\Omega_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}})\geq\lambda\quad\text{for some $\lambda>0$.}

(S102)

Assumption
S2
(
iii
) ensures the uniform boundedness of
\lambda_{\min}(\Omega_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}})
in (
S102
).
The condition on
\lambda_{\min}(\Psi_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}})
in (
S102
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}(\Psi_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}})>0,

(S103)

which we verify below by contradiction.

Proof of
\lambda_{\min}(\Psi_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}})>0
in (
S103
).

Given
\Psi_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}=\E(w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}^{\top})
as defined in (
S100
), if there exists a nonzero constant vector
a\neq 0
such that

\displaystyle a^{\top}\Psi_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}a\overset{\eqref{eq:psilsx_def}}{=}\E(a^{\top}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}^{\top}a)=\E\left\{(a^{\top}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}})^{2}\right\}=0,

then
a^{\top}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}=0
almost surely by Lemma
S6
(
i
).
This implies
a^{\top}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}v_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}^{\top}=0
almost surely, so that

\displaystyle a^{\top}\Gamma_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}\overset{\eqref{eq:gnlsx}}{=}\E(a^{\top}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}v_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}^{\top})\overset{\textup{Lemma~\ref{lem:basic}\eqref{it:lem_basic_as}}}{=}0

by the definition of
\Gamma_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}=\E(w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}v_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}^{\top})
in (
S98
) and Lemma
S6
(
i
). This contradicts (
S99
).
Therefore,

\displaystyle a^{\top}\Psi_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}a=\E\left\{(a^{\top}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}})(a^{\top}w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}})^{\top}\right\}>0\quad\text{for any nonzero constant vector $a$},

which implies
\lambda_{\min}(\Psi_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}})>0
.

S4.1.2.3
Proof of Lemma
S2
(
v
) with
s=2
.

Under Assumption
3
,
(u_{i},v_{i},w_{i})=(Y_{i},v_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}},w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}})
are identically distributed across
i\in\mathcal{I}
, so Lemma
S2
(
vi
) is satisfied. It suffices to verify Lemma
S2
(
v
) for
s=2
:

\E(Y_{i}^{4})<\infty,\quad\E(\|v_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4})<\infty,\quad\E(\|w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4})<\infty.

First, Assumption
S2
(
ii
) ensures
\E(Y_{i}^{4})<\infty
.

Next, it follows from

\|v_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\|^{2}=1+D_{i}^{2}+\|X_{i}\|^{2}\leq 2+\|X_{i}\|^{2}\leq 2\,(1+\|X_{i}\|^{2}),

(S104)

that

\displaystyle\|v_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:wilsx_2}}{\leq}

\displaystyle 4\cdot(1+\|X_{i}\|^{2})^{2}\overset{\textup{\text{Cauchy--Schwarz}}}{\leq}4\cdot 2\,(1+\|X_{i}\|^{4}),

\displaystyle\E(\|v_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\,\left\{1+\E(\|X_{i}\|^{4})\right\},

which ensures
\E(\|v_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4})<\infty
under Assumption
S2
(
iii
).

Identical reasoning after replacing
D_{i}
to
Z_{i}
ensures
\E(\|w_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\|^{4})<\infty
.

S4.1.3
Verification of the expression of
V_{{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}},N}
in Theorem
S1
(

).

From (

) and (

), under (

‣

where

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\Omega_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\overset{\eqref{eq:psinlsx}}{=}N^{-1}\displaystyle\sum_{g=1}^{G}\cov\left\{\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
Z_{i}\\
X_{i}\end{pmatrix}R_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\right\},\\
\Gamma_{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}\overset{\eqref{eq:glsx_def}}{=}\begin{pmatrix}1&\mu_{D}&\mu_{X}^{\top}\\

the last equality in the expression of

under Assumptions

.
By Lemma

\displaystyle V_{{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}},N}=(0,1,0_{p}^{\top})\Sigma_{N,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\begin{pmatrix}0\\

1\\
0_{p}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}.

Combining this with (

\displaystyle V_{{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}},N}

1\\
0_{p}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

Z_{i}\\
X_{i}\end{pmatrix}R_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\right\}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}-e\\
1\\
0_{p}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

Z_{i}\\
X_{i}\end{pmatrix}R_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{(\sigma^{2}_{Z}\cdot\pi_{\textup{c}})^{2}}\cdot N^{-1}\sum_{g=1}^{G}\var\left\{\displaystyle\sum_{{i\in\mathcal{I}_{g}}}(Z_{i}-e)R_{i,{\textup{2sls}\textup{-x}}}\right\}.

S4.2
Proof of Theorem
S2

Recall from Proposition
2
that
Z_{i\mid(C,X)}
denotes the residual from
\texttt{ols}(Z_{i}\sim C_{i}+X_{i})
, with

\displaystyle S_{ZY\mid(C,X)}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid(C,X)}Y_{i},\quad S_{ZD\mid(C,X)}=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}Z_{i\mid(C,X)}D_{i}.

(S111)

Applying Lemma
S10
with
D_{i}^{*}=D_{i}
,
Z_{i}^{*}=Z_{i}
, and
